No children, shared edits, staging, commits or publication; no dependency on active I122/I123. Mathematical proof, evidence reproduction and substantiality are separate judgments.

[400]
## 1. Independent audit of ORIGINAL I121

[401]
Verdicts below concern the original assertions, including their express limitations. No missing estimate has been silently added. The inherited I118 and I115 scoped passes stand. **Both original I112 literal failures stand:** standalone G112 invertibility fails on a closed component because constants survive; the connected nonempty-boundary application passes. R112's proportionality theorem passes, but its final normalization sentence fails for `(lambda,mu)=(0,1),(0,2)`. Their homogeneous solution jets coincide, their operators are proportional, and their DN maps differ. Neither example refutes P112.

[402]
**N1 — PASS.** Set `m=mu`, `kappa=lambda+mu`. Admissibility gives `kappa>m/3>0`, so `a=kappa/(2m+kappa)` lies strictly between zero and one. Rotation to the tangential-normal plane gives the displayed `J`, with `J²=0`; the orthogonal tangential mode is `q`. This agrees with the full repeated-root decaying physical solution, not a diagonalization of its normal symbol.

[403]
**N2 — PASS.** The Hermitian part of J is `diag(-1,1)` and its singular values are 2 and 0. Therefore `Re<Nz,z> >= (1-a)q|z|²`, `N^-1=q^-1(I+aJ)`, and `exp(-tN)=exp(-qt)(I+atqJ)`. Uniformity is only on a fixed compact admissible coefficient range. This is accretivity, not a self-adjoint symmetrization.

[404]
**N3 — PASS.** Direct multiplication gives `a²q²[[0,4i],[-4i,0]]` for `[N*,N]`. Its two eigenvalues have opposite signs; `(1,i)` has the negative one. Consequently N2 alone supplies no positive difference of the adjoint squares. The original correctly makes no assertion excluding every possible nonnormal energy.

[405]
**N4 — PASS.** `M=I+F*F` is positive, elliptic of order zero and bounded below by one on L2. Its square roots and inverse square roots act uniformly on each fixed Sobolev scale for the fixed smooth small-ball family. One may construct them by the resolvent calculus, then differentiate the resolvent and use elliptic estimates; finitely many parameter derivatives require finitely many corresponding symbol and smoothing-remainder bounds. The original never claims uniformity over all derivative orders or all coefficient classes. `I=GM^-1/2` is an isometry and `II*=Pi` exactly.

[406]
**N5 — PASS.** Compression defines `mathscr D_i=partial_i+Gamma_i`; the derivative of I is part of Gamma. This is a connection on the quotient coordinates, not differentiation with a fixed graph.

[407]
**N6 — PASS.** Insert `Pi+(I-Pi)` between the two derivatives. The original annihilator invariance `Pi Dhat_i(I-Pi)=0` is an identity on sections, so it kills the entire second term, including differentiation of the moving projection. The remaining term is exactly `mathscr D_i mathscr D_j`. It would be incorrect to delete the normal residual merely by orthogonality; that is not the original argument.

[408]
**N7 — PASS.** Applying this identity to the accepted quotient equation produces the stated second-order compression. Scalar base principal coefficients and the output drift/conjugation coefficients remain on their original tensor indices. In particular this is not a scalar harmonic equation on three arbitrary components.

[409]
**N8 — PASS.** For `phi=Iz`, the displacement functional is `h=G*phi=M^1/2 z`. Differentiating its pairing with every physical trace gives `M^-1/2 D_el M^1/2`, with the derivative of `M^1/2` and the measure contribution in `C_el`. Physical Dirichlet traces are arbitrary displacement data, making this an operator identity. The equivalence concerns the scaled displacement-functional rows; it leaves the unscaled `S_r Q/r` obligation intact.

[410]
**N9 — PASS.** For a frozen Euclidean ball, the moving trace derivative is `r^-1 A_i+f_i N_B`. Derivatives of a constant-coefficient physical solution solve the same system; uniqueness of the interior Dirichlet problem gives `[A_i,A_j]=0`. Its sum of second derivatives is `-(kappa_1/m_1) grad div`, not zero. Geodesic pullback, frame and coefficient variations have precisely the finite-order smooth dependence asserted. The original keeps both the `s^-1` terms and angular order-one geometry remainders; it does not absorb them into the source's order-zero error.

[411]
**N10 — PASS.** Expanding `div(mu(grad u+grad u^t))+grad(lambda div u)` gives the two `partial_j mu` terms and `partial_a lambda` times divergence, in addition to `mu Delta` and `(lambda+mu) grad div`. Testing the output equation against arbitrary physical input traces gives the displayed row equation with this order of compositions. No auxiliary augmented solution is substituted.

[412]
**N11 — PASS.** With constant coefficients and fixed radius, the two compositions of input trace derivatives yield the stated `r^-1` angular operator, including `A_a^t A_b^t` on output row b. The dimensionless Lamé ratios survive one physical dilation. The claim is a retained coupling, not a proof that its full energy cannot have further cancellations.

[413]
**N12 — PASS.** Both commutator identities are product rules on sections; differentiated moving coefficients remain inside the compositions.

[414]
**N13 — PASS.** Conjugation by r gives `r partial^alpha(r^-1 A)=(partial-f)^alpha A` in fixed coordinates. The binomial rule is valid there; covariant words must keep their order. A scalar angular principal symbol permits one order of commutator reduction, whereas two matrix principal symbols need not commute. The stated adjoint contains the base-density derivative. Horizontal curvature and angular rotation are not discarded.

[415]
**N14 — PASS.** The displayed four-term error is the squared-norm comparison's exact operator, with adjoints and order retained. I checked the corresponding scalar source operation through its proof ending. Its small errors use commuting scalar angular principal symbols and fixed derivative allowances. The original claim that the physical matrix calculation has not supplied the corresponding smallness is correct. This is a scoped failure of that attempted source estimate, not a nonexistence theorem for another energy.

[416]
**N15 — PASS at that scope.** The displayed norm provides the source's first-base, first-angular and two mixed half-order strengths. It cannot, without further identities or coefficient bounds, absorb arbitrary remaining order-four angular forms with negative radius powers by a small constant. N3 supplies a signed example in the stated principal subsystem. Neither N3 nor order counting proves that every full physical combination is bad; the original expressly leaves that possibility open.

[417]
**N16 — PASS.** The cutoff commutator has the factor r, symmetric-metric cross derivative, covariant Hessian and output drift as displayed. The three constant displacement moments and the physical first-jet block are distinct from four independent augmented constant fields. The lower-strip evaluation and positive-depth estimates pay only those cutoff regions, not the unmatched core.

[418]
**F1 — PASS.** `s=c(y)r` is constant on each fixed sphere; Euclidean area is exactly `s² j d sigma`, including the actual positive pullback Jacobian. `k=sK`, `tau=s² traction_1 K` and `mathcal L=s Lambda_1^tr` retain the physical Euclidean normal and medium-1 coefficients.

[419]
**F2 — PASS.** Self-adjointness of physical traction is taken in the actual surface measure before pulling back. Since `Lambda^tr K=s^-2 mathcal L k`, Green's flux becomes `j(mathcal L k-tau)^t`. The sign agrees with evaluation for the positive fundamental solution of `P=-L`. A homogeneous interior column has `mathcal L(sw)=s² traction(w)` and cancels exactly. Transposing after incorrectly replacing surface measure by probability measure would lose j; the original does not do so.

[420]
**F3 — PASS as a sufficient condition; unresolved as an actual estimate.** The fixed-order small-ball map `mathcal L:H^(v+1)->H^v` and smooth j give the sufficient H1/L2 bound in F3. It is not proved for the continued mixed K on the core. Ordinary Dirichlet estimates concern the regular extension's traction, leaving the exterior-minus-interior Cauchy defect uncontrolled.

[421]
**F4 — PASS.** Off the diagonal, subtracting G1 gives `L_1,x H=0` and `L_2,y H=(L_1,y-L_2,y)G1`, with the second tensor-index convention and lower-seed equality retained. An annular argument has an inner-boundary term. Flatness at contact supplies spatial powers with order-dependent constants; after a single fixed dilation it supplies no power of the exponentially shrinking `r_delta(y)` at an unmatched fixed center. The mixed continuation is not identified with the global G2.

[422]
**F5 — PASS.** The Kelvin kernel plus `partial_y1^k G` satisfies the separate constant physical systems off the diagonal, including differentiated divergence constraints. It is smooth at every positive separation. It does not have the required original lower seed.

[423]
**F6 — PASS, including the limitation.** Differentiating Green evaluation through a fixed surrounding surface gives `u+partial_1^k u`. The real polynomial `e3 Re((x1-y1)+i(x2-y2))^k` is divergence-free and harmonic, has kth derivative `k! e3` at the center and trace norm at most `C_k r^k`, also on the actual small geodesic spheres. Duality forces a density loss at least `c_k r^-k`. The toroidal traction calculation gives `mu(l-1)` and `-mu(l+2)`, hence Wronskian `mu(2l+1)`. Constants and lower moments do not prevent these modes. UCP excludes adding them while preserving the open seed; they are not an admissible P112 counterpair.

[424]
**F7 — PASS.** Actual output divergence is `h=sum_i D_i^el F_i`, `Q=rh`, with `J=S_rQ`, `b=K_rQ`. The stated representative follows from the exact orthogonal decomposition; boundary zero divergence is not asserted to be interior zero divergence for variable coefficients.

[425]
**F8 — PASS as the sharp scalar criterion; unresolved as a constructed gain.** Under bounded Q, D118 makes bounded minimal scalar-row norm equivalent to bounded `J/r`. Pairing J with unit vectors in `ker B_r` gives the stated pointwise operator formulation. For an integrated base estimate it is the corresponding integrated row norm, not a uniform-in-base assertion inferred from L2. The y derivative fixes the local input solution before trace density is invoked.

[426]
**F9 — PASS as explicitly proposed sufficient budgets; unresolved analytically.** The differentiated quotient route requests six weighted base derivatives and angular orders chosen to support its actual compositions. The simpler displacement Taylor continuation needs only `chi F` in L2; the augmented route also needs b and J/r. The moment route separately retains radius-independent normalization/remainders and its C45 source budget. No radius-dependent smoothness is promoted to these bounds.

[427]
**F10 — PASS.** Differentiating S and commuting Lambda produces both terms as written. **F11 — PASS.** The flat-coordinate multi-index product formula and `nabla_i(J/r)=r^-1(nabla_i-f_i)J` are exact; a covariant word uses all ordered subwords and acquires curvature upon reordering. **F12 — PASS.** Differentiation includes s, both kernel variables, the Jacobi field, profile-radius derivative, frame, physical normal, traction coefficients and area. Repetition retains `s^-1` times ordered conjugated derivatives. The source's six trace derivatives, five coefficient derivatives and seven profile derivatives fit its eight-profile allowance; a new energy would owe any additional orders. Neither known cutoff region proves the unmatched-core estimate.

[428]
**Full-operation technical disposition.** The positive scopes are reproduced by the frozen Jordan calculation, positive elliptic graph calculus, quotient/annihilator invariance, exact physical differential product rules, Green's traction identity and physical multipoles. These cover the asserted variable-coefficient, fixed-Sobolev and moving-sphere scopes, not just finite matrices. They do not furnish an estimate with a new gain. The original full-operation outcome is therefore TECHNICAL, with neither uniform coercivity nor the seed-conditioned flux estimate constructed. P112 remains unproved/unrefuted. Physical transfer and expanding Runge remain **PARKED**; this report does not launch another round of either.

[429]
## 2. Source scope and overlap screen before proposing a target

[430]
The complete original I02 report and R2 review were read, including the false shadow-only implication and the lift returning only dimension four. A sphere in R5 has full four-dimensional projections and is not a Kakeya counterexample. Its lesson is to retain common incidence witnesses. I36's original-proof PASS is preserved together with the final R3 **TECHNICAL ONLY** decision: the physical-window signature count is paid by the prior routing and finite-dimensional representative operations. I36 is not one of the eight and is not nominated again. I37's supergeometric route remains parked. No variational/free-boundary branch is pursued.

[431]
**Pinned corpus Falconer contract.** [F, exact introduction](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/math/fd4aeeb2ee4fc729c18d98444fed42fd0529eeeb/preprints/The-Falconer-distance-conjecture-in-all-dimensions-September-23-2026/build/sections/00_introduction.tex) gives positive Lebesgue measure of the **unpinned** distance set for compact `E subset R^d` with `dim_H E>d/2`. Its conclusion supplies neither a good pin, an endpoint, interior, nor a smooth distance density. In the planar case its starting pair has separated s-Frostman probabilities, `s>1`, radial Lp control after restriction, and the pair filter `Gamma_N` is constant. I read the relevant preparation proofs, full regularization/multiplication, profile tests and mask scheduling, oscillatory conversion, graph estimate, state reductions, two-depth payment and final assembly/reconstruction operations through their endings. The source's shell expansion has four independently integrated endpoints and two independent angular variables. Its row and column graph degrees pay two depth costs. Even its uniformity over bounded endpoint multipliers does not change a product measure into the diagonal pin measure.

[432]
The actual [K3 introduction](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/math/fd4aeeb2ee4fc729c18d98444fed42fd0529eeeb/preprints/The-Kakeya-maximal-conjecture-in-three-dimensions-September-23-2026/build/sections/introduction.tex) has the L3 maximal bound with `delta^-epsilon`, with constants depending on epsilon. Its complete consequences section includes interpolation and specified constant-curvature/straightenable geometric applications. The [K4 introduction](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/openai/math/fd4aeeb2ee4fc729c18d98444fed42fd0529eeeb/preprints/Every-four-dimensional-Kakeya-set-has-full-Hausdorff-dimension-September-24-2026/build/sections/01-introduction.tex) and complete consequences section include higher-dimensional lower bound four, directional variants and specified curved lifts. They do not give full dimension in R5, arbitrary variable-curvature distance estimates, or a uniform measure theorem for all nonlinear phases. Constants and structural hypotheses of the straightening map remain relevant.

[433]
The **weak pinned planar finite-distance** companion gives, for each fixed epsilon, all but o(n) pins with at least `n^(1-epsilon)` exact distances. Its crowded-fibre bound has no quantitative rate. The higher-dimensional finite-distance companion gives `c_d n^(2/d)` total exact distances; the planar unit-distance companion gives some fixed power saving below 4/3 for exact equalities. Their introductions and operative distinctions were read; I do not claim to have reread all their arithmetic proofs. None is used as a lower analytic lemma here. Generic distinct radii in an arbitrarily narrow annulus show why an exact-equality bound alone is not a scale-uniform annulus estimate. That finite example is not itself an s-Frostman counterexample.

[434]
**Current primary comparisons.** Frozen current arXiv metadata and exact versioned HTML, with local mathematical text representations, accompany the bounded literature screen:

[435]
- [Bochen Liu, v3, 2603.15328](https://arxiv.org/html/2603.15328v3), *Lebesgue measure of distance sets with regular pins and multi-scale Mizohata–Takeuchi-type estimates*: for Borel planar E,F, `dim_H E>1`, `dim_H E+dim_H F>2` and `dim_H F=dim_P F`, some `x in F` has positive-measure pinned distances to E. Current metadata still identifies v3. The complete good/bad decomposition, local tube-pair counting, ball inflation and final theorem proof were read. The regular-pin condition is used quantitatively; it is not ornamental.
- [Borges–Ou–Pálsson, v1, 2607.10574](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.10574v1), *On star configurations in fractal sets*: the actual abundance-of-L2-pinned-k-stars contract has threshold `(n²+nk+k)/(2n+1)`, `k<n`, for arbitrary prescribed separated positive-mass pieces satisfying its transversality requirements. Its k-admissible graph consequence retains common vertices and distinctness. For triangles in R3 this gives the relevant 2-star threshold 17/7, not 3/2. These are contract/graph-interface readings, not an independent proof audit of the entire paper.
- [Iosevich–Yavicoli, v1, 2610.05500](https://arxiv.org/html/2610.05500v1), current October 2026: its pinned Hausdorff/lower-box and exceptional-set improvements distinguish those conclusions from positive Lebesgue measure. Its lower-box estimate permits arbitrary Frostman measures but stops short of the selected target. The exact main contracts, rather than a title, were checked; its 8,120-line proof is not represented as fully read.
- [Iosevich–Liu–Xi, v2, 1909.05171](https://arxiv.org/html/1909.05171v2) gives pinned positivity for Riemannian surfaces above 5/4. The local microlocal decomposition, good/bad interfaces and complete final deduction were checked. Generalizing the selected threshold to a variable metric would require additional operations. [Singh–Singh, v1, 2608.28711](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28711) is a current refined-decoupling repair at the planar 5/4 threshold; it is a surrounding comparison, not an accepted axiom or a new proof dependency.

[436]
This is a bounded primary comparison, not a worldwide-priority claim. Current external literature need not assume the stipulated OpenAI premises; its historical discussion does not reopen them. The full 13,223-entry/749-manuscript/10,373-original mapping and 776 PDF-text bindings are reused. Exact lexical results and title hits are evidence of search membership, **not semantic coverage of the corpus**.

[437]
## 3. Four exact candidates

[438]
**A — selected.** For every compact `E subset R²` with `dim_H E>1`, there exists `x in E` with `Leb¹ { |x-y|:y in E }>0`. This is the planar pinned Falconer positive-measure question. No regularity of E beyond compactness, no packing-dimension equality, no lower mass law, no uniform length constant, and no all-pins conclusion are imposed. F already pays the unpinned conclusion; Liu v3 pays A with regular pins. Removing regularity while retaining one pin is a concrete additional operation, not a change in display formula. The routes below attempt exactly this gap. No proof is claimed.

[439]
**B — deferred nonlinear target.** On every smooth two-dimensional Riemannian manifold, for every compact E contained in a relatively compact geodesically convex coordinate neighborhood with `dim_H E>1`, some `x in E` has positive Lebesgue measure of `{d_g(x,y):y in E}`. Geometry is fixed before frequency tends to infinity; constants may depend on the chart and finitely many metric derivatives. Fixed-chart closeness to Euclidean geometry does not make `R(d_g-|x-y|)` small as `R->infinity`. K3/K4's special straightening hypotheses do not solve this. The read 5/4 theorem is stronger than a naive 3/2 FIO transfer, but leaves this exact target. No routine-transfer nomination.

[440]
**C — deferred common-vertex target.** Every compact `E subset R³`, `dim_H E>3/2`, has a positive-Lebesgue-measure subset of R³ consisting of `( |x-y|,|y-z|,|z-x| )` for pairwise distinct, noncollinear `x,y,z in E`. Strict triangle inequalities are required. F's one-edge result cannot be multiplied three times while retaining the same vertices. BOP already handles the appropriate graph at its stronger threshold. A planar two-star variant with arbitrary source dimension below two was rejected: the map from its one free vertex to two distances is Lipschitz, so an image of a source of dimension less than two cannot have positive two-dimensional measure. C avoids that false dimensional claim but is not developed here.

[441]
**D — deferred exceptional-pin target.** For every compact planar E with `s=dim_H E>1`, the set of pins in R² with zero-Lebesgue-measure distances to E has Hausdorff dimension at most `2-s`. This asks for a two-set positive-measure theorem without the pin packing-dimension hypothesis. Liu's regular-F contract does not imply it: Frostman does not supply a positive-mass regular restriction. The construction in §5 makes that proposed restriction shortcut false for a specific measure. D is stronger than A and remains open in this record.

[442]
All four statements avoid an endpoint assertion. Positive measure is not interior. L2 regularity is not smoothness. Constants from localization, Frostman normalization, separation, radial Lp and fixed derivative budgets are retained. Separate existential good pins do not imply simultaneous persistence; that would require a common relation or a quantitative union bound.

[443]
## 4. Route one: retain the pin in the shell reconstruction

[444]
Choose two separated positive-mass compact pieces of E carrying probabilities mu and nu with Frostman exponent `1<s<dim_H E`. This is possible by restricting a non-atomic Frostman measure to separated support neighborhoods. Finite s-energy, when needed, is obtained by first choosing a strictly larger Frostman exponent. All normalization constants are fixed before shell number N grows. In F's planar setup the pair filter can remain constant. Write `D_x(y)=|x-y|` and use its positive smooth weight `W(x,y)=D_x(y)^(-1/2)` on the separated compact product.

[445]
The useful exact bridge is the following **fibrewise monotone shell lemma**. Let (X,nu) be a probability space with standard Borel structure. Let `alpha_N,x` be jointly measurable positive finite kernels on a fixed compact interval, decreasing in N for nu-almost every x, with integrable initial mass. Let `tau_N,x` be finite complex kernels. Fix smooth low-frequency multipliers Q_N with scale `2^N`, uniformly bounded L1 convolution norms, and `P_N=Q_N-Q_(N-1)` supported in shells `A_N={2^(N-1)<=|xi|<=2^(N+1)}` (harmless fixed changes of shell widths are allowed). Suppose

[446]
\[
 \sum_N\int_X\|\alpha_{N,x}-\tau_{N,x}\|_{TV}\,d\nu(x)<\infty,
 \qquad
 \sum_N\left(\int_X\int_{A_N}|\widehat\tau_{N,x}(\xi)|^2\,d\xi\,d\nu(x)\right)^{1/2}<\infty. \tag{R1}
\]

[447]
Then `nu(dx) alpha_infinity,x(dt)` is absolutely continuous with respect to `nu(dx)dt`, and `alpha_infinity,x` is absolutely continuous for nu-almost every x. If its total mass is positive and its fibres are supported on `Delta_x E`, a positive-nu-measure set of pins has positive-Lebesgue-measure distances. The constants depend only on the chosen cutoffs and the displayed finite norms. No decay rate of `alpha_N-alpha_infinity`, no smooth density and no global L2 density are asserted.

[448]
**Proof.** All convolutions below act only in t. Plancherel and the second sum give convergence of `G=sum P_N tau_N` in `L²(nu(dx)dt)`. The first sum gives convergence of `H=sum P_N(alpha_N-tau_N)` in L1. Set `zeta_n=alpha_n-alpha_(n+1)>=0`; its integrated masses telescope and are summable. Thus

[449]
\[
 Z_M=\sum_{n\ge K}(Q_{\min(n,M)}-Q_{K-1})\zeta_n
 \longrightarrow Z\quad\hbox{in }L^1(\nu\,dt). \tag{R2}
\]

[450]
Indeed every summand has L1 norm at most a fixed constant times the mass of zeta_n, and the summands with `n<=M` have already stabilized. Finite telescoping gives

[451]
\[
 Q_M\alpha_\infty=Q_{K-1}\alpha_\infty+
 \sum_{N=K}^M P_N\tau_N+
 \sum_{N=K}^M P_N(\alpha_N-\tau_N)-Z_M. \tag{R3}
\]

[452]
The first term is L1, G is L2 and hence locally L1 on the product with any bounded t interval, and H,Z are L1. Test against a bounded measurable function of x and a smooth compactly supported function of t; approximate-identity convergence identifies the limit with `nu(dx)alpha_infinity,x(dt)`. A countable determining family in t and disintegration gives the fibre assertion. Its nonnegative density is globally integrable because the measure is finite. A nonzero positive absolutely continuous finite measure cannot be supported on a null set. This proves the lemma. It is F's entire monotone-shell argument in mixed spaces, including the non-rate telescoping term; it is a **technical extension**, not a substantial result.

[453]
**What source restoration does pay.** Define the positive fibre kernels by pushing forward `W 1_(Gamma_N)(x,y) mu(dy)`, and the approximants by also inserting F's positive mask `Theta_N(x,y)`. The source proves the integral of `W 1_(Gamma_N)(1-Theta_N)` summable. Tonelli therefore pays exactly the first sum in R1, not merely cancellation in its unpinned average. The retained product mass is positive. Smooth weights bounded above and below do not affect positivity of the distance sets.

[454]
**What source oscillation does not pay.** Put `f_N(x,xi)=hat tau_N,x(xi)` and normalize nu to mass one. The exact identity is

[455]
\[
 \int_X|f_N(x,\xi)|^2d\nu(x)=
 \left|\int_X f_N(x,\xi)d\nu(x)\right|^2+
 \frac12\iint|f_N(x,\xi)-f_N(z,\xi)|^2d\nu(x)d\nu(z). \tag{R4}
\]

[456]
The first term is the unpinned shell quantity paid by F. The second is a pin-variance term. A sufficient missing estimate is square-root summability over N of its integral on A_N. Re-expanding F's graph proof does not set this term to zero: that proof integrates `(x,x',y,y')` independently. Replacing `x'` by x places the pin measure on a diagonal and destroys the asserted independent-endpoint graph input. A delta mass at x is not an s-Frostman pin probability with uniform constants.

[457]
The uniform bounded-spatial-multiplier clause also fails to do this job by itself. For a pin partition into cells Q, random signs control a sum of squares of the **integrals** `int_Q f_N dnu`; the desired diagonal norm is the limit of `sum_Q nu(Q)^(-1)|int_Q f_N dnu|²`. The inverse masses are additional losses. Normalizing a restriction changes its Frostman constant by `nu(Q)^(-1)`; omitting that factor silently creates the unavailable uniformity.

[458]
**Counterexample to the abstract unpinning implication.** Let nu have a nonzero smooth compactly supported probability density on `(1,2)`, and set `alpha_N,x=tau_N,x=delta_x`, for every N. Monotonicity and restoration error zero hold. The unpinned average is smooth and has arbitrarily decaying Fourier shells. Every fibre is an atom, however, and its shell energy equals the shell's length, comparable to `2^N`. This is an actual family of positive kernels, not an inference from a diagram. It is not asserted to arise from the Euclidean distances of one set E. It disproves only the abstract passage from unpinned shell decay to pinned positivity.

[459]
**Route-one outcome.** R1–R3 prove the full reconstruction bridge. R4 isolates the unpaid diagonal-pin oscillation. Source restoration and regularization alone do not pay it, and no new diagonal graph estimate has been established.

[460]
## 5. Route two: keep the local pin-energy cost in tube removal

[461]
This route follows the actual point where Liu's regular-pin proof uses its hypothesis, rather than assuming that an arbitrary Frostman measure has a regular positive-mass part. In its Proposition 3.3, small-mass pin cubes are removed using a covering bound coming from packing regularity. Conditional tube bounds in the remaining cubes depend on their normalized mass. Lemma 4.4 then inflates compatible masks through prescribed intermediate scales, retaining the curvature constraint on successive radii. The final proof closes a positive power after the regularity loss is made small. I read that chain through its ending. The following calculation retains the conditional-mass cost before it is discarded.

[462]
### 5.1 A complete mass-sensitive incidence bound

[463]
Let mu,nu be compactly supported planar probabilities with separation in `[a,b]`, `a>0`. Fix a partition of the pin region into half-open squares Q of side `0<r<=1`, with `q_Q=nu(Q)>0`, and set `nu_Q=nu|Q/q_Q`. Assume `I_1(nu)<infinity`. For `0<theta<1`, take in each Q a finite catalogue of tubes T of width comparable to `theta r` and length comparable to r, in theta-separated directions, with a bounded number of overlaps at each point in each direction. Enlarged oriented tubes `tilde T` of width `C theta` and bounded length cover the corresponding rays through the separated source region. Geometry constants, including a,b and catalogue overlap, are fixed.

[464]
Let G be a pair relation such that for every T the source projection of `G intersect (T times tilde T)` has mu-mass at most `A theta`. A tube is **heavy** if `nu_Q(T)>B theta`, with `A,B>=1`. Then the mass of admitted pairs lying in a heavy tube and its extension is at most

[465]
\[
 C A B^{-1/2}\,\mathcal E_\nu(r),\qquad
 \mathcal E_\nu(r):=\sum_Qq_Q\,[rI_1(\nu_Q)]^{1/2}
 =\sum_Q[rI_1(\nu|Q)]^{1/2}. \tag{R5}
\]

[466]
**Proof.** In one direction the sum of `nu_Q(T)` is bounded by overlap, so at most `C/(B theta)` tubes are heavy. There are `C/theta` directions. For two distinct points y,z in Q, the number of catalogue tubes containing both is at most

[467]
\[
 C\min\{\theta^{-1},r/|y-z|\}\le Cr/|y-z|.
\]

[468]
The angular width restriction gives this count; bounded transverse overlap supplies its constant. Integrating gives `sum_T nu_Q(T)² <= Cr I_1(nu_Q)`. The diagonal has zero product mass since I1 is finite. A union bound, the assumed source projection bound and Cauchy–Schwarz now give, in Q,

[469]
\[
 q_Q A\theta\sum_{T\,heavy}\nu_Q(T)
 \le Cq_Q A\theta(B\theta^2)^{-1/2}[rI_1(\nu_Q)]^{1/2}.
\]

[470]
Sum over Q to prove R5. No lower mass law or dimension-regular restriction has been inserted.

[471]
The source projection hypothesis has an explicit radial way to be paid at one scale. Suppose radial pushforwards of mu at nu-almost every pin have densities f_y on the circle, with
`int ||f_y||_p^p dnu(y)<=L`, some `p>1`. Remove all pairs `(y,x)` lying in `T times tilde T` for catalogue tubes whose **full** extended source mass exceeds `A theta`. For a fixed y, such extensions lie in angular caps of size `C theta`. Their union is contained in the level set where the circular maximal function of f_y exceeds `cA`. Holder and its Lp bound give deleted source mass at most `C A^(1-p)||f_y||_p^p`. Integration costs `CL A^(1-p)`. Every remaining T has either no admitted pair or full source mass at most `A theta`, exactly the projection hypothesis above. Thus the complete one-scale bound is

[472]
\[
 (\mu\times\nu)(\hbox{discarded or admitted-heavy pairs})
 \le CL A^{1-p}+CA B^{-1/2}\mathcal E_\nu(r). \tag{R6}
\]

[473]
Using the **full** extended tube mass matters: controlling the source set separately at each pin would not automatically bound its projection over all pins in T. The argument here does not make that quantifier error. Several nested scales require the union of their removal relations and the common smooth masks used by the actual oscillatory proof; R6 alone is not that multiscale theorem.

[474]
If nu is t-Frostman with `1<t<=2` and constant C_nu, the layer-cake formula, with the integral cut off at the diameter of Q, yields

[475]
\[
 r I_1(\nu_Q)\le C_t C_\nu r^t/q_Q,
 \qquad
 \mathcal E_\nu(r)\le C_t C_\nu^{1/2}r^{t/2}\sum_Q\sqrt{q_Q}. \tag{R7}
\]

[476]
On cells satisfying the actual lower bound `q_Q>=c r^t`, their contribution is bounded by `C(t,C_nu,c) sum_Q q_Q`. An ordinary ball lower bound is not silently substituted for a lower bound on every occupied half-open dyadic cube. The latter can fail at cell boundaries. Liu's low-mass deletion is one way to pay the needed restriction. At t=1 the energy integral has a logarithmic divergence; R7 is asserted only for t>1.

[477]
R5–R7 are complete technical consequences of tube-pair counting and radial maximal bounds. The cost `mathcal E_nu(r)` is the precise term this attempt exposes; giving it a name does not pay it.

[478]
### 5.2 An actual Frostman measure with unbounded local energy cost

[479]
For each `1<s<2` construct a probability nu in the unit square as follows. At a **free** binary digit level use all four children with equal weights. At a **frozen** level use only the lower-left child. If f(n) is the number of free levels among the first n, put `m(n)=2f(n)`. Choose successive free-block endpoints b_j so large compared with the preceding block endpoint that `m(b_j)/b_j ->2`. Freeze next until

[480]
\[
 a_j=\lfloor m(b_j)/s\rfloor,
 \qquad L_j=a_j-b_j,
\]

[481]
then start the next free block. Choose the initial free block large enough and all b_j increasing. At every depth, `m(n)>=sn-O(1)`; the ratio tends to s at the frozen endpoints and to 2 at the free endpoints. Infinitely many free levels make the measure nonatomic. Every occupied depth-n square has mass `2^-m(n)`.

[482]
**Frostman and dimension proof.** A ball at scale `2^-n` meets only boundedly many depth-n squares, so `nu(B(z,r))<=C_s r^s`. Covers at a_j have `2^m(a_j)` squares of diameter `sqrt(2)2^-a_j`, giving `dim_H supp nu<=s`; the Frostman bound gives equality. Thus this is a genuine s-Frostman compact example, with `I_1(nu)<infinity`.

[483]
Every measurable positive-nu-mass set A has upper box dimension two: at a free endpoint its covering count is at least a fixed constant times `nu(A)2^m(b_j)`, and the exponent tends to two. It also has packing dimension two. Indeed in any countable bounded cover of A some covering member has positive outer nu-mass, hence upper box dimension two by the same count. The countable-cover characterization of packing dimension then gives two. As `dim_H A<=s<2`, **no positive-nu-mass restriction has equal Hausdorff and packing dimensions**. This does not forbid E from containing a nu-null subset of another kind; it specifically refutes the positive-mass regular-restriction shortcut.

[484]
At radius `r_j=2^-b_j`, all mass of each occupied Q lies in its one descendant of side `r_j 2^-L_j`. Consequently

[485]
\[
 I_1(\nu_Q)\ge (\sqrt2 r_j 2^{-L_j})^{-1},
 \qquad
 \mathcal E_\nu(r_j)\ge2^{-1/4}2^{L_j/2}
 =r_j^{-(1/s-1/2)+o(1)}\longrightarrow\infty. \tag{R8}
\]

[486]
This survives **any fixed positive-mass restriction** of nu, renormalized to probability: within each of its nonempty Q the support has the same small diameter, and the new cell masses still sum to one. These assertions use actual measures and support geometry, not just a formal profile. One may put a smooth source probability in a separated disk, making the source radial Lp bounds harmless while preserving this pin-energy obstruction.

[487]
Thus a uniform bound for `mathcal E_nu(r)` on arbitrary s-Frostman pins is false, including after fixed positive-mass restriction. This counterexample is not a counterexample to A or D: R5 is a sufficient upper estimate that can overcount. At the concentration scales it is conceivable that an adaptive incidence argument, extra oscillation, or a new profile payment could do better. None has been proved here.

[488]
### 5.3 The remaining multiscale obligation

[489]
For `A=R^epsilon` and `B=R^delta`, R6 costs
`CL R^(-epsilon(p-1))+C R^(epsilon-delta/2) mathcal E_nu(r)`.
The gain is available when the actual local-energy cost fits that budget. R8 prevents imposing a uniform constant on all intermediate pin scales. Simply skipping a long frozen interval changes the ball-inflation ratio and its curvature/mask requirements; it is not a free deletion of a line from the proof. F's regularization does retain the full mass profile, but its two-depth payment is attached to independently integrated endpoint energies. It has not been shown to pay the diagonal-pin energy spikes in the multiscale Mizohata–Takeuchi estimate.

[490]
A sufficient next operation would be a common-mask, pin-diagonal shell estimate satisfying R1 for arbitrary separated s-Frostman source/pin measures, or a multiscale tube-removal and inflation estimate that explicitly pays costs such as R8 and still leaves a summable shell gain. These are sufficient routes, not conditions asserted necessary for every proof of A. No compactness argument here produces a quantitative power. Neither the source's `delta^-epsilon` maximal estimate nor finite exact incidences supplies this missing operation automatically.

[491]
## 6. Counterchecks, significance and decision

[492]
The selected theorem is existential. An **all-pins** substitute is false even for compact sets of dimension greater than one: take a zero-length Cantor set `C subset [1,2]` of dimension a in `(0,1)`, and
`E={0} union {r omega:r in C, omega in S¹}`. Polar coordinates on bounded separated charts give dimension `1+a`, but `Delta_0 E=C union {0}` has zero length. The isolated pin is in E; no assertion about a positive-mass set of bad pins follows. D's proposed exceptional bound is not refuted by this one bad pin.

[493]
No scale-free numerical lower bound for the length is claimed: dilation changes length, and even normalized problems retain Frostman, separation and retained-mass constants. Exact finite distinct distances do not control approximate annuli. Positive measure does not force an interval; a generic L2 function is not smooth. Three separately chosen good vertices do not supply one noncollapsed triangle with a positive joint configuration measure.

[494]
A is a real named pinned Falconer question, and the read Liu v3 statement identifies a concrete boundary of the available positive-measure result. F's unpinned shell proof and Liu's regular-pin proof contain substantial prior operations; the bridges here were deliberately compared against them through their endings. R1–R3 are a mixed-space reproduction of an existing reconstruction. R5–R7 retain a cost in an existing tube-pair argument. R8 is a classical variable-branching construction used to falsify that cost's alleged uniformity. None independently clears the project's substantiality gate. The fresh work does isolate an analytic obstruction distinct from P112 and I37, but **A, B, C and D are not proved by this report; A is not refuted**. No finding is nominated.

## 1. Audit of the ORIGINAL I118 assertions

[545]
Verdicts apply to the original words and formulas, including their explicit conditional scopes. A PASS below never changes an assumed uniform bound into a constructed one.

[546]
**Inherited original I115 audit: retain all scoped PASS verdicts.** A115 uses exact local physical boundary-symbol recovery and flat pasting, not interior collar agreement. The original residual/gauge identities use the actual 17-dimensional Hessian space, including its unrestricted scalar Hessian column. F115 uses a fourth-order closure with coupled lower-order terms; the degree-three kernel remains eight-dimensional. H115 requires an open physical response comparison, not contact of images. Its determinant/conformal/UCP argument uses positive `mu` to fix scale. Fixed-radius transfer retains its stated coverage scope, actual divergence, actual traction, and no shrinking-core bound. The graph/annihilator, boundary signs, cutoff defect and whole-domain collar budget retain their original scopes. The complete nested proofs, rather than only the parent summaries, were read.

[547]
**Preserved literal failures.** G112's standalone invertibility assertion fails on a compact manifold with a closed component: constants on that component remain in the Dirichlet kernel. Its connected, nonempty-boundary application passes. R112's proportionality theorem passes, but its last sentence fails for equal zero lambda: `(lambda,mu)=(0,1),(0,2)` have proportional operators and identical homogeneous solution jets with scale two. Their DN maps differ. Neither example refutes P112.

[548]
**Small-ball family and balanced graph, (3.1)–(3.3): PASS without repairs.** On each fixed finite set of Sobolev and parameter orders the pulled-back physical Dirichlet inverse is uniformly bounded down to zero radius. Its limiting operator is the constant Lamé operator, not the scalar Laplacian. Differentiating that inverse controls the smoothing remainder as well as the boundary symbol. Hence `B_r=r T_B`, `F_r=Lambda^-1 B_r` have the asserted orders and smooth parameter dependence. The identity-plus-positive operator `M_r=I+F_r^*F_r` is invertible on L2; ellipticity and regularity extend this to each fixed Sobolev scale. With `G_r g=(g,F_r g)`, `Pi_r=G_r M_r^-1 G_r^*` is exactly the orthogonal graph projection. The orthogonal complement is `(-F_r^*a,a)`. Undoing balancing gives the original graph and annihilator, with bilinear transposes for evaluation and Hermitian adjoints only for norms. Parameter bounds after substituting the profile allow powers of beta. They are not unweighted center derivative bounds. The output factor `R=diag(I3,r)` produces the displayed unscaled lower-left `r^-1`; the test `g=omega` verifies that this loss is real.

[549]
**Frozen normal symbol and the scoped obstruction, (4.1)–(4.3): PASS without repairs.** Put `m=mu`, `kappa=lambda+mu`, `q=|eta|`, `v=(i eta,-q)`. Direct substitution of `exp(i eta.z'-qt)(a+t b)` gives

[550]
\[
D=\frac{2m}{2m+\kappa}v^t a,\qquad
b=\frac{\kappa}{2mq}vD,\qquad
\mathsf N=qI-N,\quad N=\frac{\kappa}{(2m+\kappa)q}vv^t.
\]

[551]
Here `v^t v=0`, but `vv^t` is nonzero, so `N^2=0` and the normal symbol is not diagonalizable. A positive Hermitian self-adjoint symmetrizer would make it similar to a Hermitian matrix, a contradiction. This excludes precisely that symmetrizer and an order-zero perturbation of harmonic normal differentiation. It does not exclude accretive estimates, traction energy, or a nonnormal estimate for the full quotient. For the original polynomial, the correction has Laplacian `-6 kappa e1/(3m+kappa)` and divergence `6m z1/(3m+kappa)`. Thus the physical sum of derivative squares is not zero; this is an actual solution witness.

[552]
**Ambient extension, output conjugation and projected equation, (4.4)–(4.7): PASS without repairs.** With unscaled augmentation the small-ball rescaling tends to the Laplacian, so an unrestricted auxiliary Dirichlet extension exists on sufficiently small balls. This is used only to differentiate the physical graph. The product rule for `W=diag(I3,r Lambda^-1)` gives exactly `Chat_i=(partial_i W)W^-1+W C_i W^-1`. A moving angular frame also differentiates Lambda. Conjugating the output by R gives `-2 f_i E4`, the rightmost `-f_i R A_i R^-1 E4`, and `(|df|^2-Delta f)E4` with the original signs and order. The top-right drift is `kappa/(mr)`. It survives constant freezing and physical dilation.

[553]
Differentiating a physical transfer tests the output equation against physical inputs only. Smooth traces of solutions on slightly enlarged physical balls are dense in the physical graph, by smooth Dirichlet dependence. If n is a smooth annihilator section, differentiating its zero pairing against every such input proves `Pi Dhat_i(I-Pi)=0` as an operator on sections. Repetition gives the second-order identity. Thus the quotient equation is valid. There is no inference that an arbitrary augmented extension solves the second equation.

[554]
**All projection and cutoff commutators, (4.8)–(4.11): PASS without repairs.** On `Pi phi=phi`, the normal part is exactly `(I-Pi)[Phat,Pi]phi`. The first commutator is `partial_i Pi+[Chat_i^t,Pi]`. Applying the product commutator rule twice gives all three compositions in (4.9); neither factors nor differentiated coefficients may be commuted. The outer radius commutes with Pi. Output coefficients act on the other tensor index and commute with this input projection. Orthogonality gives (4.10). The cutoff formula follows from two differentiations of the scalar cutoff; on covariant base tensors it is the corresponding covariant Hessian contraction. These formulas do not bound the normal part, and hence do not imply coercivity of the compression. The cutoff assertions are only local-evaluation bounds on the lower strip and fixed-separation bounds at positive depth.

[555]
**Right inverse and uniform Sobolev perturbation, (5.1)–(5.4): PASS without repairs.** The two normalized equations are `(l+3)a+2l b=1` and `2a+(4l+2)b=-kappa/m`; their determinant is `2(2l+3)(l+1)`. The original coefficients solve them, including `l=0` with `a=1/3`. They and their fixed coefficient-parameter derivatives are O((l+1)^-1). Multiplication by omega shifts harmonic degree by one, proving the stated one-order Sobolev gain. The Neumann inverse is first taken on one fixed scale; elliptic regularity and bijectivity there give the inverse on every other fixed scale. Radius thresholds may depend on the finite collection of requested bounds. This is not an assertion of one threshold uniform over arbitrary derivative orders or coefficient classes.

[556]
Duality using the right inverse on `H^-1 -> L2` gives `||a||H1 <= C||B_r^*a||2`. Coercivity of its form and elliptic inversion therefore justify `(B_r B_r^*)^-1`, `K_r`, and the orthogonal projection `S_r` on `ker(B_r:L2->H^-1)`. For variable coefficients this is zero **boundary** divergence, exactly as the original warns; zero interior divergence is not inferred.

[557]
**Sharp quotient criterion D118, (5.5): PASS without repairs.** For `Q=rh`, write `J=S_r Q`, `a=K_r Q`. The decomposition `Q=J+B_r^*a` supplies the upper estimate with `(p,b)=(J/r,a)`. Conversely `Q=rp+B_r^*b` gives `J/r=S_r p`, `a=rK_r p+b`. Uniform L2 boundedness of K proves the lower estimate. These identities extend distributionally to an arbitrary admissible L2 pair; no implicit H1 hypothesis on b is added. Integration over the base preserves the constants. The necessity conclusion concerns the scalar row in precisely this unscaled graph-dual norm. Bounded Q pays a, but not J/r. The toroidal high-degree example disproves a finite-moment inference only; it is not claimed to be the continued flux.

[558]
**Local evaluation and geodesic weights, (5.6)–(5.10): PASS without repairs.** At frozen coefficients divergence is harmonic; its center value is its spherical average, and the volume divergence theorem gives `Qev_0=B_0^*1=3 omega^t` for the unit Euclidean ball with probability sphere measure. Smooth dependence on independent center/radius parameters makes `Qev_r-B_r^*1` a smooth function vanishing at r=0. Its divided difference is smooth by the integral Taylor formula, which proves every fixed parameter derivative assertion. The frozen displacement polynomial formula and its degree-two moment give exactly the displayed alpha and beta2.

[559]
The geodesic statement is valid with its express weight qualification. For `gamma=e^{-2Lt0} dx^2`, a parallel orthonormal parametrization has `X(y,r,omega)=y+c(y)r O(y)omega+O(r^2)`, `c=e^{Lt0}`. In a frame with O removed, `r div_x=c^-1 div_z` at r=0. With a reference measure `dmu=w_y d sigma`, the frozen mean weight is `w_y^-1`; with probability rotation measure it is 1. The frozen row is then `3c^-1 omega^t/w_y`. The physical boundary divergence trace carries the same factor. Surface Jacobians and frame factors are pulled back before this identity is used. They are smooth and positive, so the divided-difference proof survives. The original did not claim that a geodesic surface area measure is Euclidean. None of this establishes a bound for a two-medium continued kernel.

[560]
**Actual flux identities, (5.11)–(5.13): PASS without repairs.** Transposing physical traction in the flux gives the displacement density F. Differentiating the transfer through a fixed surrounding surface, then pulling back the sphere measure, gives `h=sum_i(partial_i F_i+(C_i^el)^t F_i)` and `Q=rh`. Thus its scalar output is actual output divergence. The splitting in (5.12) represents this same functional. Differentiation of S and the angular commutator must remain, and `r partial^alpha(J/r)=(partial-f)^alpha J` is the exact conjugation identity in flat coordinates. Covariant versions retain curvature. The coefficient `r^-1` in C is differentiated as well. These are finite-radius identities, not boundedness claims.

[561]
**Conditional T118, including source frames, contact and globalization: PASS without repairs.** Four actual physical cap-source responses form an invertible augmented value matrix V1 at contact by J112; equality of all response jets from the matched side gives the same invertible V2 there. Continuity supplies fixed invertible frames nearby. With B=V2 V1^-1, Taylor against the assumed unscaled representative yields `|U2,F-B U1,F| <= C_F r rho`. Every compact set in the fixed core `t<0` has `sup r ->0`. Its L2 error tends to zero under the hypothesized L2 bound for rho, with no subsequence or compactness claim for GL(4). The resulting equality is smooth and holds for every source separately. H115 with coordinate identity forces the physical comparison and coefficients to agree.

[562]
The source depth is `integral_(1/8)^(t0) exp(-2Ls) ds`, so it is **strictly negative at contact**, not zero. The shrinking neighborhood crosses the frontier. The physical maximal match glues by literal equality, its Green equality propagates off one pole, and a ball in the match touching its complement supplies a smooth one-sided contact away from the source ball. A local extension contradicts maximality. Connectedness fills the enlarged interior; continuity covers its closure. No inaccessible boundary step is omitted. The hypothesis must hold at every such frontier. It is not supplied by fixed-radius product continuation.

[563]
**Simpler displacement condition: PASS without repairs.** Constants solve both variable-coefficient physical systems. Reproduction from the known side and UCP give `integral F=I3` exactly. Therefore a uniform L2 bound for the actual F makes `u2,F-u1,F` vanish by first-order Taylor on the shrinking core. Actual divergences agree by differentiation of the resulting exact equality. Physical jets and R112, normalized by positive mu on the known side, determine the coefficients. This condition suffices without an unscaled divergence-row bound; the original correctly calls it unavailable.

[564]
**Remaining conditional budgets: PASS without repairs as bookkeeping, unresolved as constructed estimates.** The original requests radius-independent normalized moments/remainders, their fixed high smooth bounds, and the unscaled estimate before invoking interpolation. In dimension three, `d*=23/2`, `eta=1/23`, `p*>23`, `m>=45` give the stated orders ten, eight, and nine in the F115 chain. The first-jet block has 15 coordinates per output row modulo its single divergence gauge. It is not four independent constant augmented solutions. Physical spatial dilation uses `(u_epsilon,d_epsilon)=(u(p+epsilon z),epsilon d(p+epsilon z))`; the 3D Green displacement has factor epsilon and differentiated tensor factors acquire their corresponding additional powers. One fixed dilation precedes delta shrinkage. These observations validate the conditional statements, not their missing analytic antecedents.

## 3. Attempt one: accretivity and an exact compression to displacement coordinates

[570]
This attempt goes beyond simply noting a Jordan block. It proves a positive frozen estimate, removes the extrinsic projection residual exactly by changing quotient coordinates, and derives the signed principal terms that still prevent applying the source estimate.

[571]
### 3.1 A positive nonnormal estimate, with its limitation

[572]
At a nonzero tangential covector rotate so `eta=q e1` and put `a=kappa/(2m+kappa)`, so `0<a<1`. On the tangential-normal two-plane,

[573]
\[
\mathsf N=q(I-aJ),\qquad
J=\begin{pmatrix}-1&-i\\-i&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad J^2=0.
\tag{N1}
\]

[574]
The remaining tangential direction has symbol q. Thus

[575]
\[
\operatorname{Re}\langle\mathsf N z,z\rangle
\ge(1-a)q|z|^2,\qquad
\|\mathsf N^{-1}\|\le q^{-1}(1+2a),\qquad
 e^{-t\mathsf N}=e^{-qt}(I+atqJ).
\tag{N2}
\]

[576]
Proof: `(J+J*)/2=diag(-1,1)`, `||J||=2`, and the nilpotence gives both the inverse and exponential explicitly. These estimates are uniform on a fixed compact admissible coefficient range. No Hermitian matrix making N self-adjoint has been introduced. The decaying half-space branch can therefore be estimated despite its Jordan block.

[577]
But its adjoint-square commutator is

[578]
\[
[\mathsf N^*,\mathsf N]
=a^2q^2\begin{pmatrix}0&4i\\-4i&0\end{pmatrix}.
\tag{N3}
\]

[579]
It has eigenvalues `+4a^2q^2` and `-4a^2q^2`. The vector `(1,i)` realizes the negative sign. Hence positivity in (N2) does not give a positive difference of raising/lowering squares. Dilation changes neither a nor this principal matrix. This proves a concrete limitation of this energy attempt, without excluding some different nonnormal coercive estimate.

[580]
### 3.2 Eliminate the normal residual algebraically, without discarding it

[581]
Work in a real Hilbert identification and complexify afterwards. All maps below act on each input row; output indices are separate. Let

[582]
\[
M=I+F_r^*F_r,\quad Gg=(g,F_rg),\quad
I_r=GM^{-1/2},\quad I_r^*I_r=I,\quad I_rI_r^*=\Pi.
\tag{N4}
\]

[583]
The positive square root and inverse are uniformly order zero on each fixed Sobolev scale. This follows either from positive elliptic functional calculus or its resolvent construction with the uniform spectral lower bound 1. All required fixed parameter derivatives follow by differentiating the resolvent; no radius-independent bound over infinitely many orders is asserted.

[584]
Set `phi=I_r z`, and define the compressed connection on three-component input rows by

[585]
\[
\mathscr D_i z=I_r^*\widehat D_i(I_r z)=\partial_i z+\Gamma_i z.
\tag{N5}
\]

[586]
The identity `Pi Dhat_i(I-Pi)=0` proved in the original audit implies, as differential operators on sections,

[587]
\[
 I_r^*\widehat D_i\widehat D_j I_r=\mathscr D_i\mathscr D_j.
\tag{N6}
\]

[588]
Indeed insert `Pi+(I-Pi)` between the two D's. The second summand is exactly zero after left projection, including the derivative of its moving projection. The first summand is (N6). This is not an estimate that makes the discarded normal component small: it is an exact quotient-coordinate identity. Accordingly the compressed equation is

[589]
\[
\mathscr P z=r\{a^{ij}\mathscr D_i\mathscr D_j+
 \widehat b_2^i\mathscr D_i+\widehat A_2^0\}z=0.
\tag{N7}
\]

[590]
Here `a=gamma^-1`; `widehat b` includes the fixed covariant drift and the conjugation terms from (4.5). They are retained, with the same scalar conformal multiplier applied to the output equation.

[591]
There is a useful way to check every coefficient of (N5). If h is the displacement functional represented by phi, then `h=G^*phi=M^{1/2}z`. Let `D_i^el=partial_i+(C_i^el)^t` be the exact physical displacement trace connection, including the measure derivative. Equality of pairings for all physical traces gives

[592]
\[
\mathscr D_i=M^{-1/2}D_i^{el}M^{1/2},\quad
\Gamma_i=M^{-1/2}(\partial_iM^{1/2})
 +M^{-1/2}(C_i^{el})^tM^{1/2}.
\tag{N8}
\]

[593]
For the scaled output, h consists of the actual F rows and Q row. Thus bounded z is equivalent to bounded **scaled** displacement functionals, not to the unscaled augmented norm. D118's `S_r Q/r` remains necessary in that formulation. The coordinate change has not removed the physical loss.

[594]
### 3.3 The full leading physical operator and derivative accounting

[595]
In a flat frozen ball write `A_i g=(partial_i u_g)|S`, `N_B g=(omega.partial u_g)|S`. Then exactly

[596]
\[
C_i^{el}=r^{-1}A_i+f_iN_B,\qquad
[A_i,A_j]=0,\qquad
\sum_iA_iA_i=-\frac{\kappa_1}{m_1}\,(\nabla d_{u_g})|_S.
\tag{N9}
\]

[597]
For the last formula the right side denotes the boundary operator on g. The derivative solutions solve the same constant physical system, so Dirichlet uniqueness proves the first commutation identity. The second is its actual PDE, not a scalar harmonic identity. On the conformal geodesic spheres, the Euclidean leading terms use physical length `s=c(y)r` and the smooth frame. Parameter differentiation and the pulled-back Dirichlet inverse give additional matrix angular order-one terms with coefficient O(1), bounded geometry terms, and the retained profile factors; terms of size `s^-1` must still be kept. Such an O(1) order-one remainder is already stronger than the scalar source's order-zero difference, and is not silently absorbed here.

[598]
To display the output coupling without auxiliary choices, the actual displacement density satisfies the exact flat-coordinate equation

[599]
\[
0=\mathcal P_{el}F_a:=\frac r{m_2}\left[
 m_2\sum_iD_i^{el}D_i^{el}F_a+
 \kappa_2\sum_bD_a^{el}D_b^{el}F_b+
 \sum_j(\partial_jm_2)(D_j^{el}F_a+D_a^{el}F_j)
 +(\partial_a\lambda_2)\sum_bD_b^{el}F_b\right].
\tag{N10}
\]

[600]
All operators act on the input row on their right; the subscripts a,b label output components. This follows by differentiating the flux against every local physical displacement input, and density of their arbitrary displacement traces. It is a genuine equation, not a projected equation, but it has the nonscalar physical output principal part. In the constant, fixed-radius model its pure angular leading term is

[601]
\[
r^{-1}\left[\sum_i(A_i^t)^2F_a+
 \frac{\kappa_2}{m_2}\sum_b A_a^tA_b^t F_b\right].
\tag{N11}
\]

[602]
It cannot be removed entrywise. It is the same coupling represented by the `r^-1` drift in (N7). Physical dilation leaves the ratios in (N11) unchanged. A bound for each N alone does not control their coupled raising operator.

[603]
Here is the complete rule set used to retain the base/angular/scaling derivatives; it is also an explicit specification of the unpaid terms, rather than an ellipsis treating them as harmless:

[604]
\[
[\mathscr D_i,Z]=\partial_i Z+[\Gamma_i,Z],\qquad
[\mathscr D_i\mathscr D_j,Z]
=\mathscr D_i[\mathscr D_j,Z]+[\mathscr D_i,Z]\mathscr D_j,
\tag{N12}
\]

[605]
\[
\partial^\alpha(r^{-1}A)
=r^{-1}(\partial-f)^\alpha A,\quad
[\partial^\alpha,Z]=\sum_{0<\nu\le\alpha}{\alpha\choose\nu}
 (\partial^\nu Z)\partial^{\alpha-\nu},\quad
[UV,Z]=U[V,Z]+[U,Z]V.
\tag{N13}
\]

[606]
For angular `Z=Lambda^s`, scalar principal symbols permit the usual one-order reduction when commuting with a single matrix operator. Between two matrix angular operators there is **no such reduction unless their principal symbols commute**. In base measure `mu(y)dy`, `mathscr D_i^dagger=-partial_i-partial_i log mu+Gamma_i^*`. Derivatives of `M^±1/2`, `r`, `c`, the angular frame, the surface density, all output coefficients and the fixed conformal drift occur in (N8), (N12) and (N13). In a horizontal frame retain `[nabla_i,nabla_j]` and its angular rotation action. The equations are invariant under switching to flat coordinates with the corresponding connection coefficients, so these rules enumerate the entire finite expansion at any prescribed order.

[607]
In particular, if one tries the source's squared-norm comparison, its exact error operator remains

[608]
\[
2[T^\dagger,U]+2[U^\dagger,T]+[U^\dagger,U]
 -2(UZ+(UZ)^\dagger).
\tag{N14}
\]

[609]
For the source these reduce to forms of types `epsilon beta^-1 Psi^1 partial_y^2`, `epsilon Psi^2 partial_y`, `epsilon beta Psi^3`. Here (N3), (N8), and (N11) leave matrix principal products with no justified one-order reduction or small factor. Simply retaining their order-four form would require angular H2 control and coefficients with negative radius powers. The source norm

[610]
\[
\mathcal N(z)^2=\int\{|\nabla z|_0^2+\beta^2|z|_1^2
 +\beta^{-1}|\nabla z|_{1/2}^2+\beta|z|_{3/2}^2\}
\tag{N15}
\]

[611]
does not bound that form by a small multiple. (N3) supplies actual signs in one principal subsystem; it is not merely an order-count warning. Cancellations in a newly designed full physical energy could change this conclusion, but none is proved here.

[612]
Normalization is kept physical. Constants give the three exact displacement moments. A first-jet normalization uses a fixed finite source right inverse on the 15-dimensional physical first-jet space, modulo one divergence gauge per output row. Such a block must be transported through (N8); ordinary four-component mean-zero is not invariant under Pi or M. The cutoff commutator of (N7) is exactly

[613]
\[
[\mathscr P,\chi]
=r\{2a^{ij}\chi_i\mathscr D_j+a^{ij}\nabla_i\nabla_j\chi
 +\widehat b_2^i\chi_i\}.
\tag{N16}
\]

[614]
Both cutoff regions are paid as in the original audit. The shrinking-core estimate is not.

[615]
**Attempt-one outcome.** Accretivity (N2) is proved and exact compression (N6) removes the need to estimate an extrinsic normal component separately. It leaves a full nonnormal physical operator, the singular coefficient (N11), the physical normalization block and the matrix principal terms in (N14). No uniform coercivity for that operator has been proved. This is a technical reformulation with a genuine positive frozen estimate, not a successful A* construction.

[616]
## 4. Attempt two: bound the actual Cauchy defect of the continued kernel

[617]
This attempt estimates the physical Green flux directly and then tests whether its divergence can satisfy D118. It does not substitute a local evaluation kernel.

[618]
### 4.1 An exact scaled flux identity

[619]
Use M's actual parametrization `X(y,r,omega)=exp_y^gamma(r omega)`. Set `s=c(y)r` with `c=e^{Lt0}` and write the **Euclidean physical** surface density as

[620]
\[
dS_x=s^2 j(y,r,\omega)d\sigma_y,\qquad j>0.
\]

[621]
The entire smooth Jacobian j is retained; s is a scalar at each center. Let K be the top physical block of the continued mixed kernel, let `Lambda^tr` be the physical displacement-to-traction map on this geodesic ball, pulled back to the sphere, and define

[622]
\[
k=sK(X,y),\quad \tau=s^2\sigma_{1,x}(K)(X,y)\nu,
\quad \mathcal L=s\Lambda^{tr}.
\tag{F1}
\]

[623]
Column by column, self-adjointness of traction for the actual surface measure gives the exact density

[624]
\[
 F_\delta=j\,(\mathcal L k-\tau)^t.
\tag{F2}
\]

[625]
Indeed `Lambda^tr K=s^-2 mathcal L k`; multiplying by `s^2j` in the two terms of Green's flux proves (F2). Transposing with respect to probability measure without j would be wrong. The coefficients are those of medium 1 and the normal is the actual Euclidean outward normal. This identity applies on the conformal geodesic sphere, not only on a Euclidean sphere.

[626]
Uniform small-ball Dirichlet estimates give `mathcal L:H^(v+1)->H^v` at each fixed Sobolev order. Consequently a sufficient kernel estimate for the simpler displacement condition is

[627]
\[
\sup_\delta\{\|\chi k\|_{L^2_yH^1_\omega}
                  +\|\chi\tau\|_{L^2}\}<\infty.
\tag{F3}
\]

[628]
The exact, weaker quantity to bound is `chi j(mathcal L k-tau)^t`. These are statements about the continued K; the usual point singularity estimates for either original Green kernel do not prove them. They are available on the lower strip and at positive depth only.

[629]
There is a precise reason ordinary Dirichlet energy cannot supply (F3). If a first-variable kernel column w extends as a homogeneous physical solution through the entire ball, then `mathcal L(sw)=s^2 traction(w)`. Its contribution to (F2) is zero. The density measures the part that fails to have such an interior extension. A Dirichlet estimate bounds the regular extension of a boundary trace, but does not identify its traction with that of the continued exterior column. An annular energy estimate retains the inner-boundary Cauchy term. Dropping it would set this very defect to zero. Positivity of the Lamé energy supplies no sign for that mixed Cauchy term.

[630]
Subtracting the first-medium Green kernel does not fix this. On the moving off-diagonal domain, `H=K-G1` satisfies

[631]
\[
L_{1,x}H=0,\qquad
L_{2,y}H=(L_{1,y}-L_{2,y})G_1,
\tag{F4}
\]

[632]
with the appropriate second tensor-index convention, and vanishes on the known-side seed. This is an exact inhomogeneous continuation problem. It has no prescribed small Cauchy data on the missing inner boundary. Flatness at contact gives arbitrary fixed powers of the **spatial** distance with constants depending on that power. After the spatial dilation is fixed, a coefficient difference at an unmatched center is fixed while `r_delta(y)` tends to zero exponentially. It cannot be bounded by `C r_delta(y)` merely from smallness of the dilation. A global resolvent identity for `G2-G1` also does not identify H: K is the mixed continuation, not globally G2.

[633]
### 4.2 Physical multipoles test the proposed estimate

[634]
The missing control is visible even with full physical product equations, beyond an abstract trace density. For one admissible constant medium let G be its Kelvin fundamental matrix for positive `P=-L`. Fix an integer `k>=1` and consider off the diagonal

[635]
\[
K^{(k)}(x,y)=G(x-y)+\partial_{y_1}^kG(x-y).
\tag{F5}
\]

[636]
It satisfies both physical equations and all differentiated physical divergence constraints. Differentiating Green evaluation through a fixed surrounding surface shows that its flux on local physical solutions is

[637]
\[
T^{(k)}u(y)=u(y)+\partial_1^k u(y).
\tag{F6}
\]

[638]
It reproduces constants, and all polynomial moments of degree less than k. It is smooth at every positive separation with every fixed derivative bounded on any fixed separated compact set. Nevertheless its displacement density is not uniformly bounded as r tends to zero. Take the actual physical polynomial

[639]
\[
u(x)=e_3\operatorname{Re}((x_1-y_1)+i(x_2-y_2))^k.
\]

[640]
It has zero divergence and zero vector Laplacian, hence solves the Lamé equation. Its center value is zero, its kth x1 derivative is `k! e3`, and its sphere trace norm is `C_k r^k`. Duality in (F6) forces `||F_r^(k)||2 >= c_k r^-k`. On the actual conformal geodesic spheres the same trace has size at most `C_k r^k`, so the lower bound persists. This uses a real physical polynomial, not a false scalar identity on all physical traces.

[641]
For another direct flux check, a toroidal harmonic of degree l has regular field `rho^l V_l` and exterior field `rho^(-l-1)V_l`. Both are harmonic, tangent and divergence-free. Their tangential tractions are `m(l-1)rho^(l-1)V_l` and `-m(l+2)rho^(-l-2)V_l`. Their integrated Wronskian is `m(2l+1) integral|V_l|^2`, independently of rho. Normalizing the regular boundary trace to V_l produces the `rho^-l` density loss. Thus the bad high modes have an actual physical flux realization.

[642]
Crucially, (F5) **does not equal the original evaluation kernel on the lower open seed**. It is not the actual K arising from P112 data; UCP precludes adding it while keeping that seed. It is therefore a counterexample only to an estimate deduced from off-diagonal equations, fixed-radius smoothness and finite moments alone. No admissible pair with equal partial DN maps has been constructed. The seed-to-core estimate that excludes these modes uniformly is exactly what remains to be proved, and cannot be replaced by this example's qualitative uniqueness observation.

[643]
### 4.3 Exact cancellation target and all derivative losses

[644]
For the actual F define the true scalar row and its split by

[645]
\[
h=\sum_iD_i^{el}F_i,\quad Q=rh,\quad J=S_rQ,\quad b=K_rQ.
\tag{F7}
\]

[646]
Then `Psi=((F,0),(J/r,b))` is the original actual unscaled representative. D118 proves that, assuming bounded Q, its scalar row has bounded minimal norm if and only if J/r is bounded. Equivalently the required estimate on the infinite-dimensional kernel is

[647]
\[
\sup_{\substack{B_rg=0\\\|g\|_2=1}}
\left|r\operatorname{div}_y S(y;u_g)\right|\le Cr,
\tag{F8}
\]

[648]
in the corresponding pointwise operator formulation (or the integrated row norm for the L2-in-base version). The derivative in y uses the local fixed-input solution u_g and a fixed surrounding surface, then density extends it to traces. Zero boundary divergence does not mean zero interior divergence for variable coefficients. There is no source identity forcing (F8) for the cross-medium flux.

[649]
Here are sufficient budgets with their precise roles. For the direct T118 conclusion only the core L2 norms `chi F`, `chi b`, `chi J/r` are required, together with the actual representation and reproduction. The simpler displacement conclusion needs only `chi F`. To run a differentiated quotient-energy construction one may instead require, for every `0<=j<=6` and the chosen fixed angular Sobolev orders v needed in the compositions,

[650]
\[
\|\chi\beta^{-j}\nabla^jF\|_{L^2_yH^v}
+\|\chi\beta^{-j}\nabla^jb\|_{L^2_yH^v}
+\|\chi\beta^{-j}r^{-1}(\nabla-d\log r)^jJ\|_{L^2_yH^v}\le C_{j,v}.
\tag{F9}
\]

[651]
This is an explicitly stronger proposed budget, not a derived estimate or a claim that differentiation is needed for the simpler Taylor limit. Choose the finite angular orders before shrinking; orders must support the actual compositions, adjoints and angular form estimates, not merely L2 multiplication. The normalization uses fixed source families with C45 bounds if the original moment/F115 path is pursued; that path still requires radius-independent D,R. Its order-ten remainder is differentiated twice to form E through eight, and the order-three ODE state gives D through nine. These facts do not follow just by bounding a radius-dependent smooth kernel.

[652]
Every derivative in (F9) expands by (N12)–(N13) and the following identities, which prevent a lost factor of r:

[653]
\[
\nabla J=(\nabla S_r)Q+S_r\nabla Q,\qquad
\Lambda^vJ=S_r\Lambda^vQ+[\Lambda^v,S_r]Q,
\tag{F10}
\]

[654]
\[
\partial^\alpha Q
=\sum_{\nu\le\alpha}{\alpha\choose\nu}
 (\partial^\nu r)\partial^{\alpha-\nu}\sum_iD_i^{el}F_i,
\quad
\nabla_i(J/r)=r^{-1}(\nabla_i-f_i)J.
\tag{F11}
\]

[655]
The multi-index sum in (F11) is in a fixed coordinate trivialization. For a covariant derivative word, use the Leibniz sum over all subwords, preserving their relative order; reordering those words adds the connection commutators. Differentiating (F2) also differentiates j, s, the pulled-back normal, coefficients, and mathcal L. For independent center/radius parameters,

[656]
\[
\nabla_i k=(\nabla_i s)K+s\{(\nabla_xK)\nabla_i X+\partial_{y_i}K\},
\tag{F12}
\]

[657]
and tau has the analogous product derivative with both powers of s and the derivative of traction. `nabla_i X` includes `r_i`, the geodesic Jacobi field and frame transport. Repeated derivatives follow the displayed product rule, with no omission of `partial_r`, angular or measure terms. Profile bounds give `|partial^j r|<=C_j r beta^j`; differentiating `s^-1` gives `s^-1` times the ordered `partial-d log s` factors. The `r^-1` part of C in (F7) is consequently still singular after any such bookkeeping. Six trace base derivatives, up to five coefficient derivatives in the squared-norm comparison, and profile derivatives through seven fit inside the original eight-profile allowance; if a new energy uses additional derivatives it must pay an enlarged finite budget explicitly.

[658]
On the lower cutoff strip the original divided-difference Poisson calculation proves the unscaled cancellation for the **actual** flux there, because that flux equals local evaluation there. On the positive-depth strip all radii and separations have fixed lower bounds. On the unmatched core neither (F3), the weaker defect bound (F2), nor (F8) or (F9) is established. A bounded scaled Q alone cannot yield the divided derivative in (F11).

[659]
**Attempt-two outcome.** The actual density is exactly the physical boundary Cauchy defect (F2). Regular extensions cancel from it; uncontrolled singular modes are not bounded by ordinary Dirichlet energy. Physical multipoles demonstrate the insufficiency of the proposed local substitutes. The exact seed-conditioned norm estimate for this defect, or the trace-kernel gain (F8) with the required budgets, remains unpaid.

[660]
## 5. Remaining analytic obstruction and decision

[661]
The geometry is fixed throughout both attempts: `gamma=e^-2Lt0 dx^2`, M's depth with lower integration limit 1/8, the geodesic sphere map, the prescribed cutoff and the strictly negative-depth core. Choose the geometry, finite derivative orders and source tests first, then p*, amplitude and positive separation margins, then **one** sufficiently small physical spatial dilation. Only after these choices may delta tend to zero. With equal normalized scalar principal metrics there is no new principal-metric bootstrap to use to make the physical Lamé coupling small; its nonzero frozen ratio remains.

[662]
There are two precise potential ways to reopen the branch. One is a seed-normalized coercive estimate for (N7) or equivalently the actual physical operator (N10), controlling the nonnormal principal commutators and (N11), on the proper physical normalization block, with the source's half-order strengths or a fully proved replacement. The other is a uniform estimate for `chi j(mathcal L k-tau)^t` for the uniquely continued mixed kernel. If only scaled augmented bounds are available, the additional sharp requirement is `chi S_r Q/r` and its needed derivatives. These are sufficient analytic inputs; they are not declared logically equivalent to P112 or necessary for all possible proofs.

[663]
If the displacement estimate closes, the exact constant normalization, Taylor limit, physical jet rigidity, cap normalization and maximal-match argument audited above complete the proof. If the unscaled augmented estimate closes, the fixed four-source frames and conditional T118 complete it; alternatively the original moment/F115/H115 route must pay all its stated normalization and derivative budgets. Neither antecedent has been constructed here, so no unconditional continuation theorem is nominated.

[664]
The boundary expanding-Runge branch remains parked. Its whole-domain budget retains both `I_Ct(w1,w2)` and `I_Ct(u1,u2)`, every support commutator, the physical `grad chi.u` divergence defect, opposite Carleman boundary signs and the divergence remainder `O(tau^(2-N))` multiplied by every loss. No new estimate controls the signed inner collar flux. Small collar volume, individual energy bounds, or accessible flatness does not pay it.

[665]
After the two attempts above, further rearrangement of these same unpaid estimates is not a reason to continue. **Park the physical-transfer branch pending a new mechanism that controls the actual seed-to-core flux.** The new accretivity calculation, exact quotient coordinates and Cauchy-defect identity are technical consequences of full physical and accepted lower operations. They do not increase the accepted count. P112 has neither a proof nor an actual admissible counterpair in this record.

[666]

[693]
Original I118 positive assertions pass independent I121 audit without repairs at their stated scopes, preserving I115 scoped passes and both original I112 failures. [694]
The Hermitian part of J is diag(-1,1), J^2=0 and its norm is two. The frozen normal symbol is accretive with lower constant 1-a, yet its adjoint-square commutator has eigenvalues plus/minus 4 a^2 q^2. The vector (1,i) attains the negative sign. This does not preclude all nonnormal energy methods.

[695]
Inserting Pi+(I-Pi) between the two derivative operators gives N6 because Pi Dhat_i(I-Pi)=0 as an operator on sections. The isometric graph coordinates remove an extrinsic residual but preserve the scaled nature of h=M^(1/2)z and the unscaled S_r Q/r obligation.

[696]
The physical output equation N10 retains the gradient of mu in both symmetric-gradient terms, gradient of lambda times divergence, and the constant mixed output derivative. The frozen r^-1 angular term therefore remains after dilation. Parent checked the source squared-norm identity and the scalar-principal commutator cancellation it requires.

[697]
With k=sK, tau=s^2 traction K, mathcal L=s Lambda and dS=s^2 j d sigma, the scaled flux has density j(mathcal L k-tau)^t. A regular interior homogeneous extension cancels from this density. Ordinary Dirichlet control of the extension does not bound the difference from the exterior continued traction.

[698]
The multipole kernel G+partial_y1^k G reproduces evaluation plus the kth derivative on physical solutions. The e3 Re((x1-y1)+i(x2-y2))^k witness is divergence-free and harmonic, with boundary norm O(r^k) and center derivative k!. It violates a bound based only on equations, separation and finite moments, but fails the required lower open seed and is not a P112 counterpair.

[699]
F9 and the ordered conjugated derivative identities are proposed sufficient budgets, not established estimates. The simpler Taylor continuation needs only the actual displacement-density L2 bound. Both known cutoff regions remain controlled; neither implies the shrinking unmatched-core bound.

Complete report/program reading, selected source-interface and algebra checks, flat provenance replay and finite diagnostics; not a full independent gate for new universal analytic assertions. Two selected PDF re-extractions only.

# I118 — Original I115 audit and physical quotient angular continuation

[711]
**Disposition: original scoped I115 proofs PASS without repairs; P112 and A* remain unresolved; no substantial nomination.** The original G112 boundary-component invertibility failure and R112 equal-zero-lambda normalization failure remain failures. Two constructions below produce an explicit uniformly controlled trace quotient, an exact projected commutator residual, an explicit divergence right inverse, and a sharp norm criterion for recovering unscaled divergence from a displacement density. They do not control the actual continued density on the shrinking core. Eight accepted substantial findings remain eight.

[712]
All theorems **and lower operations** at `openai/math@fd4aeeb2ee4fc729c18d98444fed42fd0529eeeb` are accepted. No upstream truth audit, contradiction argument, or ex-falso inference is used. The target is still: on every bounded connected smooth Euclidean three-domain, equality of the static energy forms for every pair of vector displacements supported in the same arbitrary nonempty relatively open proper patch determines both real smooth Lamé coefficients, assuming only `mu>0` and `3lambda+2mu>0`. No accessible or inaccessible interior collar, geometric restriction, coefficient subclass, additional measurement, or enlarged gauge is imposed. Local small balls and finite background bounds below are tools available for each fixed admissible smooth pair, not additional hypotheses on P112.
